Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I, um, I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more, but... I guess what finally brought me in is that I just haven't been feeling quite like myself lately. 

I'm sure it's probably nothing major, but I used to be so involved in things, you know? I always made time for painting, and hiking, and my choir... even just our Sunday family gatherings. But lately, even if I have a free hour, I just kind of sit there. I just can't seem to care about any of it right now. I feel terrible even saying that out loud, it sounds so ungrateful or lazy, doesn't it? But that's sort of why I'm here. I hope that's an okay reason to come in... am I explaining that right?

Oh, um... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe... about eight months? It really started building around the time I took over the STEM-Arts curriculum initiative at work.

Oh, um... well, my appetite hasn't really changed much, I don't think. I always try to make sure I eat so my husband doesn't worry. But my sleep... I guess my sleep has been kind of all over the place for a few months now. 

I really hate to complain, because I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, but... I just lie awake at night, and my brain won't turn off. I keep replaying all these grant details and paperwork I have to help with for the curriculum. And then I end up waking up really early, usually around three or four in the morning, and I just can't seem to settle back down. 

Even on the rare nights when I do manage to sleep a little longer, I still get up feeling like I never really rested at all. I just wake up feeling so drained. I'm sorry, I don't want to sound like I'm making a huge deal out of nothing... I'm sure lots of people don't sleep perfectly, and maybe it's just stress from work. But I guess it has been a pretty noticeable change for me. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right information.

I don't feel that spark anymore. \n\nI just sort of sit there, going through the motions. I can't seem to actually care about it or enjoy it, even once I'm right in the middle of doing it. I feel so guilty saying that, because I know I should be having a good time, especially with my family. I really try to smile and act like everything is fine so I don't bring anyone else down. I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... that's a... a hard question to answer. I, um... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy, or ungrateful for my life, because I'm not. I really do have a good life, and a wonderful husband. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, when I'm working late in the studio, just trying to get things done for the curriculum... I really hope that makes sense... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, my appetite hasn't really changed much, I don't think. I guess my sleep has been kind of all over the place for a few months now. \n\nI really hate to complain, because I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, but... I just lie awake at night, and my brain won't turn off. I keep replaying all these grant details and paperwork I have to help with for the curriculum. Oh, um... well, I guess I do feel bad sometimes, but I'm sure everyone feels that way when they aren't doing their best. I try really hard not to dwell on it, because I know it's not helpful to complain. 

But... I suppose, lately, when things go wrong with the curriculum project at work—even just little things, like a typo or a scheduling issue—I get this awful feeling. I just keep thinking that I'm letting the students down. And... well, my parents worked so hard and sacrificed so much so I could get my degree and have a good job like this. I just feel like I'm wasting all their effort when I can't even get excited about the work or do it perfectly. 

I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terribly ungrateful of me. I really do appreciate everything I have, and I don't want to sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know I should be doing better. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting by telling you that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly.

It really started building around the time I took over the STEM-Arts curriculum initiative at work. \n\nI mean, it's a wonderful opportunity, and I'm so grateful the school trusted me with it, so I really shouldn't be complaining. I just... I think that's when I started feeling a bit overwhelmed, and the things I usually love just sort of slipped away from me. I hope I don't sound like I'm just whining about being given more responsibility. I really do want to do a good job for them. Is it normal for things to build up like that over a few months?\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the activities you're able to do, even if you're not motivated to start them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the part that really bothers me, and kind of like I was saying a few minutes ago about not feeling like myself... even if I do manage to force myself to go to Sunday dinner, or I sit down with my paints, I just... I don't feel that spark anymore. \n\nI just sort of sit there, going through the motions. I can't seem to actually care about it or enjoy it, even once I'm right in the middle of doing it. I feel so guilty saying that, because I know I should be having a good time, especially with my family. I really try to smile and act like everything is fine so I don't bring anyone else down. I'm sorry, I know I'm probably just repeating myself now... but does that answer your question? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... that's a... a hard question to answer. I, um... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy, or ungrateful for my life, because I'm not. I really do have a good life, and a wonderful husband. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, when I'm working late in the studio, just trying to get things done for the curriculum... I do catch myself having these thoughts. Just... thinking that it might be nice, or a relief, I guess, if I just... didn't wake up the next morning. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know how terrible that sounds. Please don't think I'm actually going to do anything! I haven't made any sort of plan or anything like that, I promise. It's just... the thoughts kind of pop up, and they scare me. They scared me enough that I finally had to tell my husband about them, because I just didn't think I could keep handling them all by myself anymore. I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this... I just want to be honest so you can help me. Is it normal to have thoughts like that when you're feeling so overwhelmed?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up, like work or family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I hope I'm answering this right, but... I don't really get nervous or worried about them. It's not like a panic or anything like that. \n\nIt's more just... well, like I was saying before, just feeling like I don't have the energy for it, or feeling guilty that I'm not looking forward to it. But I don't sit around stressing or fretting about the actual events beforehand. I really hope that makes sense... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, my appetite hasn't really changed much, I don't think. I always try to make sure I eat so my husband doesn't worry. But my sleep... I guess my sleep has been kind of all over the place for a few months now. \n\nI really hate to complain, because I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, but... I just lie awake at night, and my brain won't turn off. I keep replaying all these grant details and paperwork I have to help with for the curriculum. I just wake up feeling so drained. I'm sorry, I don't want to sound like I'm making a huge deal out of nothing... I'm sure lots of people don't sleep perfectly, and maybe it's just stress from work. I just want to be honest with you so you can do your job. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past eight months where you felt somewhat better, like your normal self, even if only for a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um, no, not two years. It hasn't been that long at all. I definitely wouldn't have let it go on for two years without saying something. 

It's really only been... well, I guess it started building up about eight months ago. That's when I took over the STEM-Arts curriculum initiative at school. I was so honored they asked me to lead it, and I've really tried to do a good job with it. It's just... I think maybe I put a little too much pressure on myself to make it perfect, and that's when I started feeling so... flat. 

But it's only been eight months, so it's not like a long-term, chronic thing. I'm sure it's just a temporary slump, right? I really hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill by being here. Does eight months sound like a normal amount of time to feel this way when you take on a big project? I just want to make sure I'm not wasting your time.

Oh, um... moving slowly? I don't think so. I mean, I definitely try to keep up my usual pace. With the kids at school and the new curriculum, I really can't afford to slow down, you know? I want to make sure I'm still doing a good job for everyone. 

I guess... well, maybe it takes a little more effort to get out of bed in the morning, but I always make sure I'm up and ready on time. I wouldn't want to inconvenience anyone or be late. I'm definitely not just dragging my feet all day. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be sure I'm being helpful.

Oh, um, yes, definitely. It's been way more than two weeks of feeling like this... like I said, it's been about eight months now. 

I really try to keep a smile on my face, especially at work, but... most days, I just feel like there's this heavy, gray film over everything. It's so strange. Oh, um... improved mood? No, I don't think so. I haven't really had any periods where I felt back to my old self for that long. 

I mean, there are definitely good moments here and there, like if one of my students does something sweet, or if my husband brings me a cup of tea... I always try to focus on those little things and show how much I appreciate them. I really don't want to seem ungrateful or like I'm just miserable all the time. 

But as far as a whole couple of months where the heavy feeling went away... no, I'm afraid not. It's pretty much been a constant struggle since it started. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.

I guess what finally brought me in is that I just haven't been feeling quite like myself lately. \n\nI'm sure it's probably nothing major, but I used to be so involved in things, you know? I always made time for painting, and hiking, and my choir... even just our Sunday family gatherings. But lately, even if I have a free hour, I just kind of sit there. I just can't seem to care about any of it right now. I feel terrible even saying that out loud, it sounds so ungrateful or lazy, doesn't it? But that's sort of why I'm here. I hope that's an okay reason to come in... am I explaining that right?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, like you can't seem to care about the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I guess it's been a little while now. Maybe... about eight months? It really started building around the time I took over the STEM-Arts curriculum initiative at work. \n\nI mean, it's a wonderful opportunity, and I'm so grateful the school trusted me with it, so I really shouldn't be complaining. I just... I think that's when I started feeling a bit overwhelmed, and the things I usually love just sort of slipped away from me. I hope I don't sound like I'm just whining about being given more responsibility. I really do want to do a good job for them. Is it normal for things to build up like that over a few months?\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the activities you're able to do, even if you're not motivated to start them?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the part that really bothers me, and kind of like I was saying a few minutes ago about not feeling like myself... even if I do manage to force myself to go to Sunday dinner, or I sit down with my paints, I just... I don't feel that spark anymore. \n\nI just sort of sit there, going through the motions. I can't seem to actually care about it or enjoy it, even once I'm right in the middle of doing it. I feel so guilty saying that, because I know I should be having a good time, especially with my family. I really try to smile and act like everything is fine so I don't bring anyone else down. I'm sorry, I know I'm probably just repeating myself now... but does that answer your question? I hope I'm making sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: Oh... um... that's a... a hard question to answer. I, um... I really don't want you to think I'm crazy, or ungrateful for my life, because I'm not. I really do have a good life, and a wonderful husband. \n\nBut... well, sometimes, when I'm working late in the studio, just trying to get things done for the curriculum... I do catch myself having these thoughts. Just... thinking that it might be nice, or a relief, I guess, if I just... didn't wake up the next morning. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know how terrible that sounds. Please don't think I'm actually going to do anything! I haven't made any sort of plan or anything like that, I promise. It's just... the thoughts kind of pop up, and they scare me. They scared me enough that I finally had to tell my husband about them, because I just didn't think I could keep handling them all by myself anymore. I hope it's okay that I'm telling you this... I just want to be honest so you can help me. Is it normal to have thoughts like that when you're feeling so overwhelmed?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about things that are coming up, like work or family gatherings?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I hope I'm answering this right, but... I don't really get nervous or worried about them. It's not like a panic or anything like that. \n\nIt's more just... well, like I was saying before, just feeling like I don't have the energy for it, or feeling guilty that I'm not looking forward to it. But I don't sit around stressing or fretting about the actual events beforehand. I really hope that makes sense... is that the kind of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, my appetite hasn't really changed much, I don't think. I always try to make sure I eat so my husband doesn't worry. But my sleep... I guess my sleep has been kind of all over the place for a few months now. \n\nI really hate to complain, because I know everyone has trouble sleeping sometimes, but... I just lie awake at night, and my brain won't turn off. I keep replaying all these grant details and paperwork I have to help with for the curriculum. And then I end up waking up really early, usually around three or four in the morning, and I just can't seem to settle back down. \n\nEven on the rare nights when I do manage to sleep a little longer, I still get up feeling like I never really rested at all. I just wake up feeling so drained. I'm sorry, I don't want to sound like I'm making a huge deal out of nothing... I'm sure lots of people don't sleep perfectly, and maybe it's just stress from work. But I guess it has been a pretty noticeable change for me. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your physical activity level or the way you move, like feeling slowed down or restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, besides just being a little sluggish in the mornings from not sleeping well, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I definitely don't feel restless, and I don't think I'm moving any slower than usual during the day. \n\nI always try to keep up my normal pace at work so I don't let any of the teachers down, you know? So I think my physical activity is mostly the same. I hope that's okay... I just haven't really noticed my body feeling any different in that way. Is it alright that I don't have that symptom? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information and being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past eight months where you felt somewhat better, like your normal self, even if only for a few weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. I mean, I've just kind of been feeling this way steadily for a while now. \n\nI really try to put on a brave face, especially for my husband and the kids at school, so maybe to them it looks like I'm having good weeks here and there. But on the inside, it's just been this same... heavy feeling, I guess, without really any breaks. \n\nI'm sorry, I know it probably sounds like I'm just being negative, and I really don't mean to be. I always try to look for the bright side of things. It's just that I haven't really felt like my old self in quite a long time. I hope it's okay that I haven't had any of those better periods... I just want to be honest with you so you can do your job. Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty for not enjoying the things you used to, or criticize yourself for not being able to care about them?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I guess I do feel bad sometimes, but I'm sure everyone feels that way when they aren't doing their best. I try really hard not to dwell on it, because I know it's not helpful to complain. \n\nBut... I suppose, lately, when things go wrong with the curriculum project at work—even just little things, like a typo or a scheduling issue—I get this awful feeling. I just keep thinking that I'm letting the students down. And... well, my parents worked so hard and sacrificed so much so I could get my degree and have a good job like this. I just feel like I'm wasting all their effort when I can't even get excited about the work or do it perfectly. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terribly ungrateful of me. I really do appreciate everything I have, and I don't want to sound like I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I know I should be doing better. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not overreacting by telling you that... I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My weight has stayed pretty much the same, I think. \n\nI mean, I always try to make sure my family is eating healthy meals, and I usually just eat whatever I make for them. I might not always finish everything on my plate lately, but I don't think it's made a difference on the scale. I definitely haven't gained or lost a significant amount. \n\nI hope that's the right kind of answer? I know sometimes weight changes can be a big deal, so I just want to reassure you that everything is perfectly fine in that department. Is it okay that my weight has been stable? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the details you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual or like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed feeling restless or... or moving any slower than usual. \n\nI mean, I still get up and get the kids ready for school, and I go to work and do my job... I just try to keep up with everything the best I can. Sometimes I might feel a little tired, but I think that's just normal when you're a working mom, right? Everyone gets a bit worn out. I definitely don't think I'm pacing around or, you know, sluggish in a way that would bother anyone.\n\nI really try to keep my energy up so I don't slow anyone else down. Does that sound normal to you? I hope I'm answering that right... I just want to make sure I'm not making a big deal out of normal tiredness.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I try to keep busy, you know? I want to make sure everyone else is happy and taken care of, so I focus a lot on that. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... that's actually the main reason I finally decided to make an appointment. I just... I don't really feel like myself lately. I used to always make time for things I loved. I'd go hiking, or I'd paint, and I was in a choir... and I always looked forward to our Sunday family gatherings. I loved hosting them. \n\nNow, though... even if I magically find a free hour to myself, I just... I just sit there. I can't seem to muster up the energy to care about any of those things anymore. I just feel so blank. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I really should be trying harder to enjoy my life, especially with my family right there. I hope I don't sound ungrateful or lazy. I just... I want to get back to being the person who does those things, you know? Is it okay that I'm struggling with this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way, without any significant improvement, for more than two years?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, not two years. It hasn't been that long at all. I definitely wouldn't have let it go on for two years without saying something. \n\nIt's really only been... well, I guess it started building up about eight months ago. That's when I took over the STEM-Arts curriculum initiative at school. I was so honored they asked me to lead it, and I've really tried to do a good job with it. It's just... I think maybe I put a little too much pressure on myself to make it perfect, and that's when I started feeling so... flat. \n\nBut it's only been eight months, so it's not like a long-term, chronic thing. I'm sure it's just a temporary slump, right? I really hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill by being here. Does eight months sound like a normal amount of time to feel this way when you take on a big project? I just want to make sure I'm not wasting your time.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this persistent lack of interest in activities for most of every day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, yes, I suppose it is most of the day. But I really try not to let it show, you know? I still get up and do everything I'm supposed to do. \n\nIt's just... like I mentioned before, the main reason I'm here is because I just don't feel like myself. I used to be so active. I'd make sure I had time for my painting, and I loved going hiking... I was in a choir, and I was always the one organizing our big Sunday family dinners. I really prided myself on that. \n\nBut now... it's just this constant feeling, all day, every day. Even if I do manage to get a quiet hour to myself, I just... sit there. I can't seem to care about painting or the choir or any of it. It's like the joy has just been drained out of me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I'm repeating myself a bit. I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right. It's not that I'm trying to be difficult or ignoring my hobbies on purpose... it's just this heavy feeling that won't go away, no matter what time of day it is. Does that make sense? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to, or like it's harder to get started on things?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... moving slowly? I don't think so. I mean, I definitely try to keep up my usual pace. With the kids at school and the new curriculum, I really can't afford to slow down, you know? I want to make sure I'm still doing a good job for everyone. \n\nI guess... well, maybe it takes a little more effort to get out of bed in the morning, but I always make sure I'm up and ready on time. I wouldn't want to inconvenience anyone or be late. I'm definitely not just dragging my feet all day. I hope that's the right answer? I just want to be sure I'm being helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, yes, definitely. It's been way more than two weeks of feeling like this... like I said, it's been about eight months now. \n\nI really try to keep a smile on my face, especially at work, but... most days, I just feel like there's this heavy, gray film over everything. It's so strange. I can be standing right there in the middle of a classroom full of my students, or sitting on the couch at home with my husband, and I still just feel completely hollow inside. And... well, I feel like I'm close to tears a lot of the time, even when nothing is really wrong. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that probably sounds terribly dramatic, and I really don't want to come across as a complainer. I know I have a good life and I should just be grateful. But it has definitely been a constant feeling for well over two weeks. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're asking about? I hope I'm not oversharing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of improved mood over the past eight months that lasted for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... improved mood? No, I don't think so. I haven't really had any periods where I felt back to my old self for that long. \n\nI mean, there are definitely good moments here and there, like if one of my students does something sweet, or if my husband brings me a cup of tea... I always try to focus on those little things and show how much I appreciate them. I really don't want to seem ungrateful or like I'm just miserable all the time. \n\nBut as far as a whole couple of months where the heavy feeling went away... no, I'm afraid not. It's pretty much been a constant struggle since it started. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
